<pre>The question asks about the substrate that binds at the active site for thymidylate synthase activity.

Thymidylate synthase (TS) is an enzyme that catalyzes the conversion of deoxyuridine monophosphate (dUMP) to deoxythymidine monophosphate (dTMP). This is a critical step in the biosynthesis of thymidine nucleotides, which are essential for DNA synthesis and repair.

The reaction catalyzed by thymidylate synthase is:
dUMP + N5,N10-methylenetetrahydrofolate → dTMP + dihydrofolate

So the substrate that binds at the active site for thymidylate synthase activity is dUMP (2′-Deoxyuridine-5′-monophosphate).

- dTMP is the product, not the substrate.
- dCMP is not involved in this reaction.
- UMP is the ribonucleotide, not the deoxyribonucleotide substrate.

The answer is A.
